Files: `SequenceAligner.ClustalWIteration.cs`, `SequenceAligner.ClustalWMultiple.cs`, `SequenceAligner.ClustalWProtein.cs`, `SequenceAligner.GuideTree.cs`, `AlignmentTypes.cs`, `AlignmentTools.cs`, `docs/mcp/tools/alignment/multiple_align_clustalw.md`, csproj, oracle TSV + generator.

### F103 — ALIGN-MULTI-001 (audit A2-14, part 4): ClustalW profile alignment commands `-PROFILE` and `-SEQUENCES` ported, 1 200 / 1 200 rows equal the installed binary
- **Gap.** The library had no profile-alignment entry point (F92: "a profile-alignment entry point (`-PROFILE`, `-SEQUENCES`) that the library does not have yet").
- **Source (opened).** `Clustal.cpp` (`profileAlign`, `sequencesAlignToProfile`), `multipleAlign/MSA.cpp` (`doProfileAlign`, `calcPairwiseForProfileAlign`, `seqsAlignToProfile`), `tree/TreeInterface.cpp` (`getWeightsForProfileAlign*`, `getWeightsFromDistMat*`, `readTreeAndCalcWeightsNJ`), `pairwise/FullPairwiseAlign.cpp` + `FastPairwiseAlign.cpp` (pair ranges `pairwiseAlign(.., 0, n, newSeq - 2, n)`), `general/SymMatrix.h`, `alignment/Alignment.cpp`.
- **API.** `SequenceAligner.AlignClustalWProfiles(profile1, profile2, ClustalWOptions | ClustalWProteinOptions)` (`-PROFILE1 -PROFILE2 -PROFILE`) and `SequenceAligner.AlignClustalWSequencesToProfile(profile, sequences, options)` (`-SEQUENCES`); rows may contain `-` (gapPos2); result rows: first input, then second; MCP `clustalw_profile_align`, `clustalw_sequences_to_profile` (+ docs, README, tool maps, catalog 517 tools); `SequenceAligner.ClustalWProfile.cs`, `ClustalWGappedPairwiseDistance` (pairwise stage on rows with gaps: row lengths for the gap opening, residues for the identity normalisation, `res2 != gapPos2` quirk of `tracePath`).
- **What the programs do (all reproduced).** `-PROFILE`: identities inside each profile truncated to int (`static_cast<int>(countid)`), guide trees from the first-k block of the matrix (the F102 defect: the tree of profile 2 is built from the identities of the first sequences), the initial alignment, tree weights, similarities, `doProfileAlign`. `-SEQUENCES`: `countid` for the profile and the first new sequence (float), the full or fast pairwise stage between every pair that has a new sequence, run on the GAPPED rows, the NJ tree of all rows with weights and similarities from the tree (`getWeightsFromDistMat`), then `seqsAlignToProfile` (closest sequence first, weight = tree weight x identity with NO `-NOWEIGHTS` guard, unlike `multiSeqAlign`). `-ITERATION=TREE` with either command is refused: `iterationOnTreeNode` runs with `profileNum != 0`, so `Alignment::profile1NumSeqs` becomes 2 while `prof1Weight` has one element - an out-of-range read of a `std::vector` (undefined behaviour); `-ITERATION=ALIGNMENT` is not applied by either command.
- **Reference cross-check.** `scripts/alignment/generate_clustalw_profile_oracle.py`: the installed `clustalw` 2.1 on **1 200 sets (300 each of -PROFILE / -SEQUENCES x DNA / protein**; the profiles are `clustalw -ALIGN` alignments with gaps; profile 2 of -SEQUENCES unaligned, in 25 % an alignment) with `-GAPOPEN -GAPEXT -MAXDIV -NOWEIGHTS -NEGATIVE -QUICKTREE -PWGAPOPEN -PWGAPEXT -PWMATRIX -TRANSWEIGHT -MATRIX -NOPGAP -NOHGAP -GAPDIST`: **1 200 / 1 200 rows equal** (first run). The two documented MCP examples equal the binary too.
- **Tests.** `SequenceAligner_ClustalWProfile_Tests` x4, MCP `ClustalWProfileToolsTests` x3, contract test +2 tools. Files: see above + `docs/mcp/tools/alignment/clustalw_profile_align.*`, `clustalw_sequences_to_profile.*`.
- **Remaining ClustalW command-line features (registered DOABLE in the worklist, A2-14b/c/d):** guide-tree files `-NEWTREE` / `-USETREE` (and `-NEWTREE1/2`, `-USETREE1/2` of the profile commands), `-OUTORDER=ALIGNED`, the secondary-structure gap-penalty masks (`-NOSECSTR1/2`, `-SECSTROUT`, `-HELIXGAP`, `-STRANDGAP`, `-LOOPGAP`, `-TERMINALGAP`, `-HELIXENDIN/OUT`, `-STRANDENDIN/OUT`) and `-CLUSTERING=UPGMA` with `-ITERATION` (needs the user-run `-O0` binary).

### F104 — ALIGN-MULTI-001 (audit A2-14b): ClustalW guide-tree files `-NEWTREE` / `-USETREE` (+ profile variants) and `-OUTORDER=ALIGNED` ported; rows, row order and guide-tree files equal the installed binary on 3 300 sets
- **Gap.** The library could neither write the guide tree file, nor align along a user tree, nor list the rows in the program's `-OUTORDER=ALIGNED` order (the default of the program; every earlier oracle used `-OUTORDER=INPUT`); the entry points did not take sequence names.
- **Source (opened: Debian `clustalw` 2.1+lgpl-7 `src/`).** `Clustal.cpp` (`align`, `doGuideTreeOnly`, `doAlignUseOldTree`, `profileAlign`, `sequencesAlignToProfile`, `useExistingGuideTree`), `tree/Tree.cpp` (`readTree`, `createTree`, `insertNode`, `reRoot`, `insertRoot`, `calcMean`, `createSets`, `groupSeqs`, `markGroup1/2`, `calcSeqWeights`, `calcSimilarities`, `skipSpace`), `tree/TreeInterface.cpp` (`getWeightsAndStepsFromTree`, `getWeightsFromGuideTree`, `getWeightsForProfileAlign*`), `tree/ClusterTreeOutput.cpp` (`printPhylipTree`, `twoWaySplit`), `tree/UnRootedClusterTree.cpp` (`treeFromDistMatrix`), `multipleAlign/MSA.cpp` (`multiSeqAlign`, `seqsAlignToProfile`: `newOutputIndex`), `interface/CommandLineParser.cpp` (the else-if chain of the verbs, the tree options), `general/UserParameters.cpp`; libstdc++ `num_get` for `file >> dist`.
- **API (additive).** `ClustalWSequence`, `ClustalWRunOptions` (`UseTree`, `UseTree1/2`, `GuideTreeOnly`, `OutputOrder`), `SequenceAligner.RunClustalW` / `RunClustalWProfile` / `RunClustalWSequences` (DNA and protein overloads) returning `ClustalWRunResult` (`ClustalWAlignment` with rows in input order + `OutputIndex`, guide-tree texts), `SequenceAligner.ClustalWGuideTreeText`, `ClustalWTreeFileException` / `ClustalWTreeFileProblem` (`WrongFormat`, `NotCompatible`, `NameNotFound`, `UnrootedWithoutDistances`, `Undefined`), `ClustalWOutputOrder`; files `SequenceAligner.ClustalWTrees.cs` (writer, char-level reader, `groupSeqs`), `SequenceAligner.ClustalWCommands.cs`; `CreateSets` of the state replaced by the faithful `groupSeqs` port (one implementation for NJ and user trees).
- **What the program does (all reproduced).** (1) `printPhylipTree`: `fixed setprecision(5)` branch lengths (printf, exact binary value rounded half to even: `ClustalWFixed`), children listed by their lowest leaf index, three subtrees under the first parenthesis, two sequences `(a:d,b:d);` with `d` = `%.5g` of the FLOAT half distance. (2) `-NEWTREE1/2`: the name is ignored - the files are `<profile>.dnd` - both trees are written and the run stops; `-SEQUENCES` writes `<profile 2>.dnd`; `-ALIGN -USETREE=x` does not read `x` (it overwrites it): the option is `-USETREE` alone. (3) `Tree::readTree` is a hand-written character reader with the quirks of `std::ifstream`: after EOF every read fails, `skipSpace` is a no-op then, `>> dist` follows libstdc++ `_M_extract_float` (`1e` fails, `.5` and `1e-2` work, failbit zeroes the value and ends the tree); a third `,` calls `insertNode` (the new node is its own parent until `setInfo` at the end of the call; the parent keeps pointing at the old node); names are compared lower-case with blanks as `_`, truncated at 150, the LAST leaf of that name wins; a tree without branch lengths sets `distanceTree = false` (equal weights, and the PROTEIN profile stage picks the single matrix BLOSUM40 / PAM120 / GONNET250), an unrooted one without distances is refused. (4) `Clustal::profileAlign` fills the identities inside the profiles only while profile 1 has no user tree (the filling of profile 2 sits inside that `if`): with `-USETREE1` alone the tree of profile 2 is built from an all-zero matrix. (5) `doAlignUseOldTree` runs no pairwise stage; the tree is read with the NJ reader even for `-CLUSTERING=UPGMA`. (6) `-OUTORDER=ALIGNED`: `multiSeqAlign` records the sequences in the order in which they first entered a profile alignment (first pass by tree step and sequence index, then the delayed ones closest-first); `seqsAlignToProfile` the profile, then the added sequences closest-first; `doProfileAlign` the identity.
- **Undefined behaviour of the program (typed).** A tree the reader was not made for sends the program into undefined behaviour: text that ends inside a name (the reader reads past the end of the file for ever - 9 oracle sets hang), a node reachable from two branches after a nested trichotomy (`clearTree` frees it twice: 20 of 23 such sets of a stress run crash or hang, 3 survive), more than `3 (n + 1)` nodes or `n + 1` leaves (heap overflow: the "tree not compatible" message in about two thirds of the stress sets, a crash in one third), single-child nodes `((a),b)` (28 of 52 sets give rows - reproduced exactly - 24 crash). `ClustalWTreeFileProblem.Undefined` refuses the first three classes; the test accepts any refusal for the 21 hang / crash rows. Residual in the committed 3 300 sets: 3 sets on which the program survived a tree with a shared node that the port refuses as `Undefined`, and 3 crash rows (single-child nodes) on which the port returns rows; the other 18 of the 21 hang / crash rows are refused.
- **Reference cross-check.** `scripts/alignment/generate_clustalw_commands_oracle.py`: the installed `clustalw` 2.1 on **3 300 sets** (`align` 660 = 330 DNA + 330 protein: rows, row order and `.dnd` file; `newtree` 660: `.dnd` only; `usetree` 660 with random valid trees of every shape - rooted, unrooted, no distances, odd number spellings, extra whitespace, mixed case - and mutated / malformed ones: 15 `WrongFormat`, 115 `NotCompatible`, 35 `NameNotFound`, 65 `UnrootedWithoutDistances`, 12 crash, 9 hang; `profile` 660 and `sequences` 660 with user trees, the written profile trees, `-NEWTREE1/2` trees-only runs; options `-GAPOPEN -GAPEXT -MAXDIV -NOWEIGHTS -NEGATIVE -QUICKTREE -MATRIX -PWMATRIX -TRANSWEIGHT -NOPGAP -NOHGAP -GAPDIST -PWGAPOPEN -PWGAPEXT`; names of 1-14 characters with upper case, digits, `_ . -`): **every row, order, tree text and refusal equal** (21 hang / crash rows: refusal accepted). Development: first run 80 / 80 for the NJ tree text and 80 / 80 user-tree rows; the only fixes were the zero-distance quirk of (4) and the `Undefined` classification.
- **Tests.** `SequenceAligner_ClustalWCommands_Tests` x19 (the five oracle blocks, the five-sequence worked example from the binary, two-sequence tree, case-insensitive names, seven typed refusals, input validation, `ClustalWFixed` ties), MCP `clustalw_command`. Files: see API + `scripts/alignment/generate_clustalw_commands_oracle.py`, `clustalw_commands_oracle.tsv`. Commit 4272a312.

### F105 — ALIGN-MULTI-001 (audit A2-14, new sub-item A2-14e): the seven ClustalW alignment file formats and the whole command line; 2 611 / 2 611 files equal byte for byte
- **Gap.** No ClustalW output writer existed (CLUSTAL, GCG / MSF, GDE, PHYLIP, PIR / NBRF, NEXUS, FASTA) and no reader of the command line.
- **Source (opened).** `alignment/AlignmentOutput.cpp` (`createAlignmentOutput`, `clustalOut`, `gcgOut`, `gdeOut`, `phylipOut`, `nbrfOut`, `nexusOut`, `fastaOut`, `findRangeValues`, `printSecStructMask`, `SeqGCGCheckSum`, `nameonly`), `general/UserParameters.cpp` (defaults), `general/clustalw.h` (`PAGEWIDTH` 80, `LINELENGTH` 60, `GCG_LINELENGTH` 50, `MAXNAMESTODISPLAY` 30, `MINNAMESTODISPLAY` 10), `interface/CommandLineParser.cpp` (`checkParam`, `parseParams`, `setOptionalParam`, `findMatch`).
- **API (additive).** `ClustalWAlignmentWriter.Write(alignment, ClustalWOutputOptions, ClustalWStructureOptions)` / `DefaultExtension`, `ClustalWOutputFormat`, `ClustalWOutputOptions` (`-OUTPUT -CASE -SEQNOS -SEQNO_RANGE -RANGE -SECSTROUT`), `ClustalWCommandLine.Parse` (every option of the 2.1 line: spelling, `findMatch` prefixes, `sscanf` numbers, per-type k-tuple limits, numeric ranges, the else-if chain of the verbs, files in `files`), `ClustalWProgram.Run(arguments, files)` (command line + file texts -> output files), MCP `clustalw_command`; `ClustalWAlignment` carries names, rows, output order and the sequence weights of the alignment object (GCG prints them).
- **What the program does (reproduced).** CLUSTAL: names cut to `max(min(30, longest), 10)`, blocks of `(80 - width) / 10 * 10` columns (60), the conservation line compares with the FIRST INPUT sequence (strong / weak groups only for protein; the weak group list has 11 entries incl. `NEQHRK`), `-SEQNOS` counts the columns before `-RANGE` including gaps (`val >= 0 || val <= maxAA` is always true); GCG: checksum `sum((i % 57 + 1) * toupper(c)) % 10000` over `lastRes` characters (gaps `.`), `Weight` from the alignment's sequence weights (`weight * 100 / 1000f`, one decimal); PHYLIP: names cut to 10, blocks of 50; PIR: empty title line; GDE: `#` / `%` + name, lower case by default; NEXUS: `datatype=DNA | PROTEIN`; `-SEQNO_RANGE`: `findRangeValues` (end = start + lastRes - gaps - 1: the program passes the LAST column as the length) and the CLUSTAL name part is cut to the names width before `nameonly`.
- **Reference cross-check.** `scripts/alignment/generate_clustalw_output_oracle.py`: the installed `clustalw` 2.1 `-ALIGN -OUTPUT=<format> -OUTFILE=...` on **2 611 sets (373 per format: 330 `-ALIGN`, 43 `-CONVERT` of the unaligned input**; DNA and protein; names of 1-40 characters and with `/start-end`; `-OUTORDER`, `-CASE`, `-SEQNOS`, `-SEQNO_RANGE`, `-RANGE=m,n` in about a third each; the alignment option variants as flags, read by `ClustalWCommandLine`): **2 611 / 2 611 files equal byte for byte.** Development: first run 7 formats x 12 sets equal; at 330 sets two defects of the port: the weak group `NEQHRK` (index 8) had been left out (26 CLUSTAL files), and the CLUSTAL range name must be cut to the names width BEFORE `nameonly` (names that carry `/start-end`).
- **Tests.** `SequenceAligner_ClustalWOutput_Tests` x14 (7 formats x oracle, `-CONVERT` of 301 inputs, oracle coverage, MSF / CLUSTAL worked examples from the binary, the other formats, range, refusals), `ClustalWCommandLine_Tests` x8 (parameters, `findMatch`, structure ranges, verbs, refusals, `ClustalWProgram.Run`), MCP `ClustalWCommandToolTests` x3. Commit 4272a312.

### F106 — ALIGN-MULTI-001 (audit A2-14c): secondary-structure gap-penalty masks of the profile commands ported; 1 200 / 1 200 output files equal the installed binary
- **Gap.** `-NOSECSTR1/2`, `-SECSTROUT`, `-HELIXGAP`, `-STRANDGAP`, `-LOOPGAP`, `-TERMINALGAP`, `-HELIXENDIN/OUT`, `-STRANDENDIN/OUT` and the structure lines of the profile files did not exist.
- **Source (opened).** `fileInput/ClustalFileParser.cpp` (`countSeqs`, `getSeq`, `getSecStructure`, `clustalBlankline`), `fileInput/GDEFileParser.cpp` (`getSeq`, `countSeqs`, `getSecStructure`), `fileInput/FileReader.cpp` (`profileInput`: which mask is stored, `maxAlnLength`), `Clustal.cpp` (`calcGapPenaltyMask`, `profileAlign`, `sequencesAlignToProfile`: "ignoring secondary structure for a list of sequences"), `multipleAlign/ProfileBase.cpp` (`calcGapCoeff`: the mask branch), `multipleAlign/MyersMillerProfileAlign.cpp` (`profileAlign`: mask per profile after the switch, `addGGapsMask`), `alignment/Alignment.cpp` (`resetProfile1/2`), `alignment/AlignmentOutput.cpp` (structure lines of CLUSTAL and GDE), `general/UserParameters.cpp` (defaults: helix 4, strand 4, loop 1, terminal 2, helix in 3 / out 0, strand in 1 / out 1).
- **API (additive).** `ClustalWProfileStructure`, `ClustalWRunOptions.ProfileStructure1/2` + `Structure` (`ClustalWStructureOptions`), `ClustalWAlignment.Structure1/2` (`ClustalWAlignmentStructure`), `ClustalWProfileFileReader.ParseClustal` / `ParseGde`, writer support (`!SS_` / `!GM_` lines, `"SS_` / `"GM_` entries); `SequenceAligner.ClustalWStructure.cs` (`InitStructure`, `CalcGapPenaltyMask`, `AddGapsMask`), `ProfileBase::calcGapCoeff` mask branch in `CalcGapCoeff`.
- **What the program does (reproduced).** The FIRST `!SS` / `!GM` line of a profile file decides (SECST / GMASK); the structure line is filled over `maxAlnLength` columns (profile 2: the longest row of both profiles) with '.' / '1' defaults; a later block without the line gives "bad secondary structure format" and no structure; `calcGapPenaltyMask` turns helix 'A'/'a'/'$' and strand 'B'/'b'/'%' runs into penalties (core, terminal in / out, loop); a mask switches the variable-region, hydrophilic and residue-specific penalties off and zeroes `gdist`, multiplies the opening and extension penalty of the column by the digit; the masks follow the alignment (`addGGapsMask`, with the path of the other profile after a switch); the initial alignment of `calcPairwiseForProfileAlign` runs with the masks too (the rows of the initial alignment are kept, `resetProfile` removes nothing), so the masks carry its gaps into the final alignment; `-SEQUENCES` with more than one new sequence ignores the mask of profile 2 for the alignment but writes it; printed structure lines are shaped by `printSecStructMask` (profile 1 and 2 cut / compare their names with different limits; the GDE `GM_` loop of profile 2 stops one entry short and shows a stale buffer character).
- **Reference cross-check.** `scripts/alignment/generate_clustalw_structure_oracle.py`: the installed `clustalw` 2.1 `-PROFILE1=a -PROFILE2=b -PROFILE | -SEQUENCES` on **1 200 profile pairs (300 per command x type)**: CLUSTAL-format profiles written by `clustalw -ALIGN` and given `!SS_` / `!GM_` lines (structure only 62 %, mask only 18 %, both in either order 15 %, a line missing from a later block 5 %), 253 GDE-format pairs with `"SS_` / `"GM_` entries, `-NOSECSTR1/2`, `-SECSTROUT` (4 values), the eight gap / end parameters, the alignment option variants, outputs CLUSTAL / GDE / FASTA / PIR: **1 200 / 1 200 output files equal byte for byte** (first run; the masks change the rows of 549 of the 1 200 sets, so they are exercised).
- **Tests.** `SequenceAligner_ClustalWStructure_Tests` x9 (4 oracle blocks, coverage, hand-run worked example from the binary incl. `-HELIXGAP=9`, reader rules, GDE reader, `-NOSECSTR1`). Not covered: structure masks from RSF / EMBL feature tables and the MSF / PIR / RSF / EMBL / PHYLIP / NEXUS inputs (registered A2-14g). Commit 4272a312.

### F107 — ALIGN-MULTI-001 (audit A2-7, A2-45a, A2-8): the three residual T-Coffee differences found and fixed, the allow-lists removed; the "rooting tie" is not reachable
- **Defect 1 (2 of the 3 `KnownDifferences`: protein `-nomatch 50 -gapext -1`, protein `-seq_weight t_coffee -distance_matrix_mode idscore -tree_mode upgma`).** `profile_pair_wise` walks the sequences of a group in `lseq` order, and `recode_tree` (`io_lib/tree_util.c` L4915 / L4960) fills `lseq` with the sequence indices in ASCENDING order; the port walked them in the order of the tree walk (the smaller profile first). `fork_cl2pair_list_ext` adds single-precision terms `MIN(w1, w2) / 1000` in that order, so the last bit of a pair-list cell depends on it. Minimal case (the committed set, 7 sequences): cell (14, 20) of the merge {q000, q001, q002} x {q003, q004, q006} is the sum of nine terms 110 / 32 / 57 / 92 / 51 / 15 / 115 / 43 / 57 (exactly 22.0): tree order gives 21.999998 -> `(int)` 21, index order 22.000002 -> 22; the extra point makes an alignment path that tied with the traceback's choice win - found by perturbing every cell of the merge by +-1 (7 single-cell perturbations reproduce the program's rows; all of them lie on the two tied paths) and by the exact scan of the nine-term sum (210 512 of 362 880 orders give 22). `-nomatch 41..53` showed the window: the program equals the port's `-do_normalise 0 -nomatch 6` while the port switched to 7 at 47. Fix: `TcPairListExt` iterates `g0.Order` sorted ascending.
- **Defect 2 (the third `KnownDifference`: `clustalw_pair` on the protein pair YAGR / NSMYC).** Not the ClustalW protein port (it equals the installed binary on the pair): `t_coffee` decides its sequence type for the whole set (YAGR / NSMYC consist of `AGCTUNRYMKSWHBVD` letters = DNA), but the external `clustalw -align` guesses the type of every PAIR FILE itself (`-TYPE` omitted: the SECOND sequence, >= 85 % of A C G T U N = DNA; YAGR / NSMYC is protein). The port called the DNA stage. Fix: `TcPairJobEntries` takes `GuessClustalWSequenceType(pair)`; a pair that clustalw reads as DNA although it holds protein letters (a protein first sequence, a nucleotide second one) is scored with the 0 entries of the DNA matrix for the letters outside the IUB codes (`WithClustalWLenientLetters`, scope-limited: the public DNA entry points still reject them; 200 / 200 such pairs equal the installed clustalw).
- **A2-45(a).** `KnownDifferences`, `known.Count <= KnownDifferences.Count` and `crashed.Count <= 5` removed from `SequenceAligner_TCoffeeOptions_Tests`: every committed set is now compared exactly; a set on which the program aborts (`FAIL:rc` rows) must raise `TCoffeeAbortedException` (SIM's script overflow: the size rule predicts all 3 crashing sets and none of the others; the exception type also carries `Reason` / `ExitStatus` / `HeapDependent` for the new refusals). The 3 oracle rows of A2-7 are in the file and equal.
- **A2-8 (the "rooting tie that crashes the program" of F66).** Not reachable: the guide tree of `make_tree` is built by `guide_tree` (the ClustalW 1.x NJ routine, `util_make_tree.c`) from the integer distance matrix and read back by `read_tree`; neither contains a tie-dependent memory access (ties are resolved by strict comparisons in index order, the same as the port, which equals the program on 7 715 committed sets). Constructed ties: all sequences identical (k = 3..14, lengths 1 / 4 / 9, default, `-tree_mode upgma`, `-distance_matrix_mode no`: 144 runs, 0 crashes, 0 differences), 4 000 random tie-rich sets (1-6 residues, 1-20 letter alphabets, duplicate-heavy, 9 option mixes: 0 crashes). The only deterministic crash of the default pipeline remains SIM's script overflow; the crash seen during F66 development was that one.
- **Reference cross-check.** Installed `t_coffee` 13.41 (Debian 13.45.0.4846264+really13.41.0.28bdc39+dfsg-1): the 4 715 sets of A1-13d now 0 differences (was 3); +230 `clustalw_mixed` sets (mixed alphabets) and +260 `floatorder` sets (6-10 protein sequences, `-nomatch 40 / 52 -gapext -1`, `-seq_weight t_coffee`): all equal. Source (text only, never compiled): `io_lib/tree_util.c` (`recode_tree`), `dp_lib/util_dp_gotoh_nw.c` (`fork_cl2pair_list_ext`, `linked_pair_wise`), `util_lib/util_constraints_list.c` (`evaluate_constraint_list_reference`, `fork_relax_constraint_list`), `dp_lib/util_dp_drivers.c` (`tree_aln`, `make_tree`), method file of `clustalw_pair`.
- **Tests.** `SequenceAligner_TCoffeeOptions_Tests`: `ClustalwPair_MixedAlphabets_EveryRowEqualsTheProgram`, `NoMatchOnLargeSets_FloatSumOrder_EveryRowEqualsTheProgram`, the exact `AssertFeature` (no allow-list). Commit: 6553e7d0.

### F108 — ALIGN-MULTI-001 (audit A2-3, A2-4): the pair-wise methods `biphasic_pair`, `lalign_rs_s_pair`, `promo_pair`, `blastr_pair` ported; `clean_slow_pair`, `hash_pair`, `subop1_pair`, `subop2_pair`, `cfast_pair` reproduced as the program's refusals
- **Gap.** Only `slow_pair`, `lalign_id_pair`, `proba_pair`, `clustalw_pair` existed; `-method` with any other built-in method of `util_constraints_list.c` raised `ArgumentException`. The "rc 1 on the probe set" of A1-13d was investigated with the installed program: `clean_slow_pair` -> "ERROR: Weight Mode clean is unknown [FATAL:T-COFFEE]" + core dump (the method file asks for `WEIGHT clean`, `seqpair2weight` has no such mode); `hash_pair` -> core dump without message (`EXECUTABLE hash_pair` has no branch in the pair-job dispatcher: "THE FUNCTION hash_pair DOES NOT EXIST" goes to the suppressed local stderr); `subop1_pair` / `subop2_pair` / `cfast_pair` -> "Msubop1_pair not recognised [FATAL:T-COFFEE]" (the functions exist in `util_dp_drivers.c` but the method list has no entry). All exit with status 1 for two or more sequences (one sequence: no pair job, rc 0). `biphasic_pair` and the others run.
- **Ports (additive API).** `TCoffeePairMethod.BiphasicPair / LalignRsSPair / PromoPair / BlastrPair` (+ the five refused values), `SequenceAligner.TCoffeeBiphasicPairAlign`, `TCoffeeAbortedException(reason, exitStatus, heapDependent)` with `Reason` / `ExitStatus` / `HeapDependent` (the old SIM constructor is unchanged), `SequenceAligner.TCoffeeMethods.cs`; `-method` parses the names; MCP `methods` / `options` of `multiple_align_tcoffee` and `tcoffee_primary_library`.
  - `biphasic_pair` = `biphasic_pair_wise` (`util_dp_suboptimal_nw.c` L421-): five states (match; two deletion and two insertion states with opening x 2 / extension / 2 and opening / 2 / extension x 2 in integer division), ties by the order of the `max_int` calls (first listed wins, later must be strictly greater), and the program's traceback, which starts from the PREDECESSOR state of the best final state (`trace = t[trace][I][J]` before the loop) - a quirk, kept. Parameters as `slow_pair` (DNA dna_idmat -10 / -1; protein BLOSUM62, gap open (int)(10 x mean mismatch), -1).
  - `lalign_rs_s_pair` = `lalign_id_pair` with `ALN_MODE s_pairwise`: the scheduler (`util_constraints_list.c` L815-880) also queues the pair (x, x); `fast_pair` always gives the two alignment rows the indices 0 and 1, so SIM sees two sequences and the main diagonal is an ordinary candidate (a first attempt with SIM's `nseq == 1` upper-triangle mode failed on 28 / 60 sets and was removed). The library holds entries between residues of one sequence.
  - `promo_pair` / `blastr_pair` = `slow_pair` with `EXTEND_SEQ 1`, matrix promoter_tf1 / blosumR, GOP -60 / -20, GEP -1 / 0, `WEIGHT extend_sim`: `extend_seq` (`reformat.c` L11039-: every residue becomes the code of the dinucleotide it forms with the next UNGAPPED residue; n / x, u, other letters -> j; the last residue keeps its letter) and `unextend_seq` are applied in place to the SHARED sequence array around every job (the jobs of one method run in one process, `-n_core 1`), which is lossy: protein letters become j for good, u becomes t, x becomes n. The port keeps that array (`work`), re-extends it for the weight (identity of the extended rows x 10) and for the residue positions, and unextends again. With `-n_core` > 1 the jobs are split over processes and the corruption differs - not modelled (the oracle runs use `-n_core 1`).
- **Reference cross-check (installed `t_coffee`, 3 types x 2-7 sequences x 3-34 residues, 3 oracle files appended to `tcoffee_program_options_oracle.tsv`):** biphasic 390 sets (DNA / protein / RNA; single method, with `slow_pair`, with `@EP@` overrides, with `proba_pair` + upgma), lalign_rs_s 390, promo_blastr 405, refused 160 (every set a program failure, all raise `TCoffeeAbortedException`): **1 345 / 1 345 rows equal, 160 / 160 aborts**.
- **Sources opened (text only).** `util_lib/util_constraints_list.c` (method files L5380-5700, scheduler L700-880, `aln2constraint_list_generic`, `seqpair2weight`), `dp_lib/util_dp_drivers.c` (`method2pw_cl`, `fast_pair`, dispatcher), `dp_lib/util_dp_suboptimal_nw.c`, `dp_lib/util_dp_sim.c`, `util_lib/reformat.c` (`extend_seq` / `unextend_seq`), `util_lib/util_aln_analyze.c` / `aln_convertion_util.c` (`get_seq_sim`, `is_in_same_group_aa`), `io_lib/io_func.c` (`read_matrice`, `exon2mt`).
- **Not done (stays DOABLE):** `exon_pair`, `exon2_pair`, `exon3_pair` (`SEQ_TYPE G`: they align the sequences of `_G_` templates given with `-template_file`; `exon2` / `exon3` keep only columns whose letters are in the quirky set "subset_objOBJ-" of `aln2constraint_list`; matrix `exon2mt` = BLOSUM62 with o / b / j = 100 and zero x rows), see A2-4.

### F109 — ALIGN-MULTI-001 (audit A2-6, part): `-distance_matrix_sim_mode` residue groups of every `make_group_aa` mode and `idscore`; the "groups are made by a library method" assumption of A1-13d corrected
- **Defect.** `TCoffeeDistance.cs` threw `NotSupportedException` for a sim mode that is not `idmat*` / `*sim*` ("the residue groups of other matrices are not ported") and for one containing `idscore`; A1-13d also assumed that a library method makes the process-static groups (`is_in_same_group_aa`, `lgl`) and fixes the groups of the matrix `idmat` (T = U, J / O never equal) for the distance matrix.
- **Program (sources `aln_convertion_util.c` `get_seq_sim` L8634, `is_in_same_group_aa` L12671, `make_group_aa` L12351).** The text before the first '_' of the sim mode selects the groups: "" -> the groups of the matrix idmat; contains "sim" or == "idmat" -> one group per letter; `simple`, `mafft`, `clustalw`, `polarity`, `vasiliky`, `make_all`; any other name -> no group (nothing is similar, not even identical letters); the groups are built once, by the first call of the process. The pair jobs run in forked children, so in the main process the first call IS the distance matrix: the groups are those of the sim mode whatever the library methods are (sets of T / U letters, `-method slow_pair -distance_matrix_mode fast`: 0 / 80 differences with the sim-mode groups, 1 / 80 with idmat groups; `vasiliky` + `slow_pair`: 0 / 30 against 8 / 30). `matrixGroups` was removed from the port.
- **`idscore`.** `get_seq_sim` passes the ALIGNED rows of `-distance_matrix_mode fast` to `idscore_pairseq(s1, s2, -12, -1, blosum62mt, mode)` (the comp mode is the part before '_': sim2 / sim3 after it are ignored); the function reads `m[s1[i] - 'a'][s2[j] - 'a']`: a gap '-' in the FIRST row indexes 52 rows before the matrix (a wild pointer: segmentation fault, exit status 11, no alignment), in the SECOND row it reads 0. Measured on 220 committed sets with a first-row gap: the program died in all 208 whose first gap lies in one of the first 12 pair alignments (a < b order) and in 7 of 12 later ones (the memory before the matrix is heap data that is a valid pointer in some runs). Port: `TCoffeeAbortedException` (exit status 11) with `HeapDependent = false` for a gap in one of the first 12 pair alignments, `true` after that; `SequenceAligner_TCoffeeOptions_Tests` accepts a heap-dependent abort where the program ran through (5 of 540 sets) and nothing else. The distance matrix is also computed for two sequences (the program does), which is where the segfault happens for a pair.
- **Reference cross-check.** 805 new sets (simgroups 265: 12 modes x protein 14 / DNA 6 + `slow_pair` 25; simidscore 260; simidscore_gapless 280): all equal except the 5 heap-dependent aborts. Tests: `DistanceSimModeGroups_EveryRowEqualsTheProgram`, `DistanceSimModeIdscore_GapsInTheFirstRowSegfault_OtherwiseEveryRowEqualsTheProgram`.
- **A2-6, second part (same WP).** `-tree_mode` and `-distance_matrix_mode` accept every value the program accepts: `TCoffeeTreeMode.Cwph / Kmeans / Other` (+ `TreeModeText`) and `TCoffeeDistanceMode.Aln / Ktup2 / Slow / Cscore / Geometric / Random / Clustalw / Other` (+ `DistanceModeText`). Measured with the installed program on 8 sets per cell (DNA / protein, 2 / 3 / 5 sequences): `-tree_mode cwph` and an unknown tree mode: core dump (exit status 11) for 3+ sequences (`make_tree` knows only nj / upgma; the documentation lists cwph), two sequences use a dummy tree and ignore the mode; `kmeans`: `seq2km_tree_old` prints "Resolve Node" lines and the program ends with exit status 0 WITHOUT an alignment; `-distance_matrix_mode aln`: segmentation fault (exit 11; `aln2sim_mat` of the alignment `cl2distance_matrix` never gets); `ktup2`: "ERROR: UNSPECIFIED UNSPECIFIED" (exit 1); an unknown name: "Error: X is an unknown distance_matrix_mode [FATAL:T-COFFEE]" (exit 1). All raise `TCoffeeAbortedException` (`ExitStatus` 11 / 0 / 1); 360 new sets (treemodes 180, distrefused 180) equal. **`random`: BLOCKED with proof** - `id = rand() % 100`, and two runs of the same input with `-distance_matrix_mode random -seq_weight t_coffee` write different sequence weights (0.11 / 3.79 / 0.05 / 0.04 against 0.70 / 0.42 / 2.61 / 0.28): the program's `rand` is seeded differently on every run, so no result can be locked or reproduced. **Still DOABLE:** `slow`, `cscore`, `clustalw` (the DP score / consistency score of every pair with the RELAXED library, computed between the relaxation and the re-weighting - the port builds the tree before the library stage, so the stage order has to change; `slow` also needs `sub_aln2sub_aln_score` for scores above 100) and `geometric` (needs the `fasta_pair_wise` of A2-2).

### F110 — ALIGN-MULTI-001 (audit A2-2): the FASTA heuristics of `util_dp_fasta_nw.c` ported: `fast_pair`, `-dp_mode fasta_pair_wise | cfasta_pair_wise | very_fast_pair_wise`, the `very_fast` / `-quicktree` and `geometric` distance modes
- **Gap.** `TCoffeeDistance.cs` / `TCoffeeLibrary.cs` threw `NotSupportedException` for `-method fast_pair`, `-quicktree` / `-distance_matrix_mode very_fast | geometric` and `-dp_mode fasta_pair_wise | cfasta_pair_wise` (A1-13d-1).
- **Program (sources `dp_lib/util_dp_fasta_nw.c`, `util_dp_drivers.c` `method2pw_cl` / `fast_pair`, `util_lib/util_constraints_list.c` `seq2distance_matrix`, `util_lib/util.c` `return_mean_int` / `return_sd_int`).** `evaluate_diagonals_with_ktup`: the lower-cased rows are hashed by k-tuples of identical letters (k = 2 protein / 5 DNA for `fast_pair`), every pair of equal tuples adds the matrix cost of its residues to the diagonal `pos2 - pos1 + l1`, rows 1..l1+l2-1 are sorted by score (glibc `qsort` merge sort = stable). `flag_diagonals`: mean over the n_diag + 1 rows (row 0 included), `sd = (int) max(sqrt(sum (int(mean) - x)^2) / n, 1)` (an int!), threshold T = 0 -> best z-score / 25 stored in a `float`, the diagonals from the best down while z > T, plus 1, l2 and l1 + l2 - 1; `extract_N_diag(n, window)` for `-ndiag` / `very_fast` (20, window 5). `make_fasta_gotoh_pair_wise`: the Gotoh DP on the flagged diagonals; the arrays have l1 + l2 + 1 rows, which matters: `diag[n_diag + 1]` is 0 and the traceback may step through zero rows. `cfasta`: 20 + n x step diagonals (step = max(20, (int) ln(min(l1, l2)))) until two successive scores are equal (the returned score is the LAST `new_score`, 0 when the loop is not entered). The library-driven runs (`-dp_mode fasta_pair_wise` on profiles) score the diagonals with `evaluate_diagonals_with_clist`: every library entry between a residue of group 1 and one of group 2 adds `get_dp_cost(r1 - 1, r2 - 1)` (the residue NUMBERS are used as column indices).
- **Measured, not assumed.** `-nomatch` and `-ndiag` are copied into the CL AFTER the library methods were configured (`PW_CL = copy_constraint_list(CL)` precedes `CL->nomatch = nomatch`, `CL->fasta_step = fasta_step`), so they do not reach `fast_pair`: 0 of 510 oracle sets differ whether they are modelled or not (the first port modelled them and failed on 108 sets; removed).
- **Port (additive API).** `SequenceAligner.TCoffeeFasta.cs` (`TcDiagonalsByKtup`, `TcFlagDiagonals`, `TcExtractNDiagonals`, `TcMakeFastaGotoh`, `TcFastaPairRows`), `TCoffeePairMethod.FastPair`, `TCoffeeProgramOptions.DiagonalCount` (`-ndiag`), `Ktuple`, `TgMode`, `DiagonalThreshold`; `-quicktree` (replaces the distance mode by `very_fast` wherever it stands) and `TCoffeeDistanceMode.VeryFast`. A traceback entering a cell outside the band (`trace` = UNDEFINED) is the program's segmentation fault: `TCoffeeAbortedException` (never observed on the oracle).
- **Reference cross-check (installed `t_coffee` 13.41, `scripts/alignment/generate_tcoffee_options_oracle.py` feature `fast_pair`):** 510 sets (DNA / protein / RNA, 2-7 sequences of 8-70 residues, single method, `fast_pair,slow_pair`, `@EP@GOP / GEP`, `-ndiag`, `-nomatch`, `proba_pair,fast_pair -tree_mode upgma`): **510 / 510 rows equal**. The `very_fast`, `geometric` and `-dp_mode fasta | cfasta | very_fast` oracles are listed in F111 / F113.
- **Tests.** `FastPair_EveryRowEqualsTheProgram`, `FromCommandLine_FastaOptionsQuicktreeAndTemplates`.

### F111 — ALIGN-MULTI-001 (audit A2-6, remainder): `-distance_matrix_mode slow | cscore | clustalw | very_fast | geometric` computed between the relaxation and the re-weighting; `idscore` allow-list explained; `random` BLOCKED with proof
- **Gap.** The port built the guide tree before the library stage, so the modes that read the relaxed library could not be computed (`NotSupportedException`).
- **Program (`t_coffee.c` L5109-5240, `util_constraints_list.c` `seq2distance_matrix` L4937-5200, `util_dp_drivers.c` `make_tree`).** Order of operations: library -> `-filter_lib` -> `-relax_lib` -> `evaluate_constraint_list_reference` (`nomatch` rescaled once) -> `cl2distance_matrix` (`cscore` explicitly, every other mode through the `-weight` stage and cached in `CL->DM`) -> re-weighting (rescales `nomatch` again) -> `make_tree` (reads the cached matrix) -> progressive stage. The modes: `slow` = `pair_wise` of the pair with the MAIN dp mode on the relaxed library, `id` = `get_seq_sim` of the aligned rows, `score = (int)(score_aln / (len x 10))` then `score x 100 / do_normalise` in FLOAT (0 .. 10 for the default 1000: the tree distances are almost flat, a quirk as with `idscore`); `cscore` = `linked_pair_wise` score / (len x 10) in float, `id = score / 10`, empty library -> `idscore`; `clustalw` = no branch of its own: the generic `else` takes the DP score as identity; `very_fast` = `very_fast_gotoh_pair_wise` with `NCL->residue_index` = the relaxed library (so the diagonals are scored by library entries, matrix cost) -> 20 diagonals + shadow 5 -> banded Gotoh, `id` of the aligned rows, GOP / GEP of the distance mode (DNA -10 / -1, protein average x 10 / -1), `TG_MODE` = `-tg_mode`; `geometric` = `align_two_sequences(pam250mt, -10, -1, "fasta_pair_wise")` (TG_MODE 2, k-tuple 3, lower-case) of every sequence against the first three, `100 x (1 - euclid / sqrt(10000 x n))` in float, `score = (int) id x 10`.
- **Port.** `TcAlignDetailed` takes a `lateStage` callback that runs after the relaxation and returns tree + weights (+ the identity matrix for the generic dp modes); `TcRelaxedState`, `TcLibraryDistanceMatrix`, `TcLinkedPairRows` (the sparse DP of `linked_pair_wise` on two single sequences, score = max of the end cell), `TcVeryFastPairRows`, `TcGeometricDistanceMatrix`; `TCoffeeDistanceMode.VeryFast`; `-weight no` (`TCoffeeProgramOptions.WeightNo`) skips the distance matrix of two sequences.
- **Reference cross-check.** 1 906 new sets (slow 430, cscore 356, clustalw 340, very_fast / `-quicktree` 470, geometric 310; DNA and protein, 2-8 sequences of 6-50 residues, `-seq_weight t_coffee`, `-tree_mode upgma`, `-relax_lib 0`, `-filter_lib`, `-do_normalise 0`, `-nomatch`, `-gapopen / -gapext`, `-tg_mode`, `-distance_matrix_sim_mode`, two methods): **1 906 / 1 906 rows equal**.
- **`idscore` allow-list (5 sets that ran through where the port aborts, 1 differing set) - decided: undefined behaviour of the program, proof.** `get_seq_sim` hands the ALIGNED rows to `idscore_pairseq(s1, s2, -12, -1, mat, mode)` which reads `m[s1[i] - 'a'][s2[j] - 'a']`; a gap in the first row is index -52 of the row-pointer array `mat` (allocated at the first idscore call), i.e. the heap 416 bytes before it, whose content depends on the allocation history. Measured with the installed program on the same input: set of 8 DNA sequences with a first-row gap at pair 12+: exit status 0 for 26 of 70 working-directory path lengths (0..69 characters appended), 11 for 44; with `HOME` padded by 1 / 9 / 50 characters: 0 / 11 / 0; a 200-character path: exit status 6 (heap corruption). A set whose first gap is in one of the first 12 pair alignments: exit status 11 for 70 / 70 path lengths. So the outcome of the later sets is a function of the environment strings (heap layout) and not of the input: no criterion exists in the source or in the data; the deterministic part (a gap in the first row of one of the first 12 pair alignments always crashes) is modelled (`TCoffeeAbortedException.HeapDependent` = false), the later calls raise `HeapDependent = true` and the test allows at most 6 such sets (5 aborts the program survived + 1 differing set of 540). The differing set is a run in which the wild pointer was valid and the identity came from heap data.
- **`random` stays BLOCKED** (proof in F109: two runs of the same input write different weights).
- **Tests.** `DistanceModesOfTheRelaxedLibrary_EveryRowEqualsTheProgram`, `GeometricDistance_EveryRowEqualsTheProgram`.

### F112 — ALIGN-MULTI-001 (audit A2-4, remainder): `exon_pair`, `exon2_pair`, `exon3_pair` with genomic templates
- **Program (`util_constraints_list.c` method files L5400-5450, `util_dp_drivers.c` `seq2list` / `method2pw_cl` L641-700 / `fast_pair`, `aln_convertion_util.c` `fill_G_template`, `fix_seq_seq`, `io_func.c` `read_matrice("exon2mt")`).** `SEQ_TYPE G`: a pair job needs a `_G_` template (`-template_file list.fa` with headers `>name _G_ file.fa`; `check_seq_type` pairs only sequences that both have one) and `fast_pair` replaces `S->seq[s]` by the first sequence of the template file for the job. The exon methods are NOT the `fast_pair` branch of `method2pw_cl`: they use `dp_mode myers_miller_pair_wise` (= `slow_pair`), TG_MODE 1, matrix `exon2mt` = BLOSUM62 with the diagonal of o / b / j = 100 (`EXONVALUE_4_TCOFFEE` is read with `atoi` of its NAME, so 100) and the rows / columns of x (a < 60) zeroed in `PW_CL->M`; GOP -10 (exon, exon2) / 0 (exon3), GEP -1. The alignment of the templates is expressed in the residues of the sequences by `fix_seq_seq` (Myers-Miller with idmat -4 / -1 when the template differs from the sequence), the weight is `sim`; `exon2` / `exon3` add the weight text `sim_subset_objOBJ-`: `aln2constraint_list` takes the text after the FIRST '_' (`subset_objOBJ-`, prefix included, tested case-sensitively with `strstr`) as the letter set of both rows. A method without a pair leaves the library empty: "You have not provided any method or enough Sequences" (exit status 1).
- **Finding (the only non-obvious step).** When `fix_seq_seq` has to reconcile a template with its sequence it calls `aln2sim(B, "idmat")`, which is the first call of `is_in_same_group_aa` in the method process: the process-static residue groups of every weight of that method are then the 26 single-letter groups of `make_group_aa("idmat")` (J, O, U, X equal to themselves, T and U different) instead of the groups of the matrix idmat. Measured: `exon_pair` with templates x / y single letters: o-o, j-j, u-u, b-b, x-x identical (weight 1000), t-u, o-j, i-v different; `slow_pair` with a template file whose templates equal the sequences: J-J 830 (not equal), T-U 1000 (equal). Port: `TcProcessGroups` per method process, set by the first job.
- **Port (additive API).** `TCoffeePairMethod.ExonPair / Exon2Pair / Exon3Pair`, `TCoffeeProgramOptions.GenomicTemplates` (command line `-template_file @t0,t1,...`, empty = none), `TCoffeePrimaryLibraryEntries(..., genomicTemplates)`, `TcMatrix.ByName("exon2mt")` / `WithZeroX`; the empty library raises `TCoffeeAbortedException`.
- **Reference cross-check.** `scripts/alignment/generate_tcoffee_options_oracle.py` features `exon` / `exon_sparse` generate templates (the sequence with substitutions, deleted residues, runs of o / b / j, lower-cased stretches, 20 % equal to the sequence, optionally missing) and a template list file: 640 sets (DNA and protein, 2-7 sequences, single method, `exon_pair,slow_pair`, `exon2_pair,exon3_pair`, `@EP@GOP / GEP`, `proba_pair,exon3_pair -tree_mode upgma`; 87 sets are program failures - empty library - all raise): **640 / 640 equal**.
- **Tests.** `ExonPairs_EveryRowEqualsTheProgram` (also locks the library of `t_coffee -lib_only -out_lib` of a hand-made pair).

### F113 — ALIGN-MULTI-001 (audit A2-5): the generic dynamic programs of the progressive stage (`-dp_mode gotoh_pair_wise | myers_miller_pair_wise | fasta_pair_wise | cfasta_pair_wise | very_fast_pair_wise`) and the `-extend_mode` column scores; the refusals
- **Program (`t_coffee.c` L5060-5160, `evaluate.c` `choose_extension_mode` L7664, `residue_pair_extended_list` L4985, `fast_get_dp_cost_2` L6273, `fast_get_dp_cost` L6072, `slow_get_dp_cost` L6361, `get_dp_cost` L5672, `util_dp_gotoh_nw.c` `gotoh_pair_wise` L853, `util_dp_mm_nw.c` `diff`, `util_dp_drivers.c` `get_pair_wise_function`, `local_tree_aln`).** `-dp_mode` is copied to `CL->dp_mode`; the progressive stage calls `pair_wise(A, L->lseq, R->lseq)` (left child = profile 1, no size swap, group lists in ascending sequence order) with `CL->get_dp_cost` of `-extend_mode`: default `very_fast_triplet` = `residue_pair_extended_list` (the triplet score `sum min(w1, w2) / (sum of both lists' weights)` x normalise x SCORE_K, double arithmetic, `max_val` factor) + `fast_get_dp_cost_2` (the `-max_n_pair` (10) pairs of sequences of the two columns with the highest identity of the distance matrix - qsort, visited from the end - averaged over the pairs with two residues, minus SCORE_K x `CL->nomatch`); `slow_triplet` = every pair; `triplet` = `get_dp_cost` (nomatch subtracted twice); `fast_triplet` = `fast_get_dp_cost` (hashes of the library partners, `max_ext_value`). `gotoh_pair_wise` treats the first / last row and column as terminal (`-tg_mode`), picks the substitution when it ties with the insertion (`best_int` + the `sample == 0` rule), the trace stores run lengths (`eg`, `ddg[j]`); `gotoh_pair_wise_test` forces gop 0 / gep -1 and the order insertion, substitution, deletion; `myers_miller_pair_wise` is the `slow_pair` routine on column scores.
- **Port (additive API).** `SequenceAligner.TCoffeeDp.cs`: `TCoffeeDpMode`, `TCoffeeExtendMode`, `TCoffeeProgramOptions.DpMode / ExtendMode / MaxNPair / DiagonalThreshold / DiagonalCount / TgMode`, `TcDpContext` (cost functions), `TcGotohProfile`, `TcMyersMillerProfile` (the existing Myers-Miller state now takes a cost delegate and returns `midc`), `TcProfileDp` (fasta family on profiles), `TcAlignNodeDp`, `TcDpPairRows` (so `-distance_matrix_mode slow | clustalw` follow the main dp mode).
- **Reference cross-check.** 2 070 sets (`dp_gotoh` 800, `dp_mm` 480, `dp_fasta` 790; DNA and protein, 2-8 sequences of 6-50 residues, with `-gapopen / -gapext`, `-tg_mode 0 / 2`, `-nomatch`, `-extend_mode slow_triplet / triplet / fast_triplet`, `-max_n_pair 0 / 2`, `-seq_weight t_coffee`, `-relax_lib 0`, UPGMA, `-distance_matrix_mode fast`, two methods, `-do_normalise 0`, `-outorder aligned`, `-ndiag`, `-diag_threshold`): **2 068 / 2 070 rows equal**; the two others are program aborts (exit status 6) of `gotoh_pair_wise`: a one-byte heap overflow (valgrind: "Invalid write of size 1 ... 0 bytes after a block of size 104" in a `strcpy`, i.e. the terminator of an alignment row, `sprintf(aln[row], "%s", char_buf)`) that kills the process when the final alignment is 8 (mod 16) columns long (24, 40, 56; 13 of 20 sets of 3-8 DNA sequences with a 23-residue longest sequence and a 24-column alignment die, all 6 / 300 random sets that died had such a length; `biphasic_pair_wise` and `glocal_pair_wise` write their rows the same way and die in the same way, 6 of 550 sets): the port returns the alignment; the test checks the abort against that necessary condition (alignment of 24+ columns; at most 12 sets).
- **`relative_triplet` is undefined behaviour:** `residue_pair_relative_extended_list` adds `residue_index[s1][r1][a + WE]` with `a` running over the list of s2 - a read past the array when the second list is longer (58 of 60 and 59 of 60 sets of the oracle reach such a read; assuming zeros reproduces only 52 of 120 sets). The port raises `NotSupportedException` there and equals the program on every set where it does not (340 `dp_extend` sets in all: `rna0` = `slow_triplet`, `pc`, `mixt` and `relative_triplet` with the linked DP have no effect).
- **Refusals (measured on five sets of 2-6 sequences each, `dp_refused` 720 sets, all raise):** `-dp_mode idscore / subop1 / subop2 / proba / pavie / domain / fasta_cdna / cfasta_cdna_pair_wise` exit status 11, `gotoh_pair_wise_lalign` 6, `seq_viterbi / hh / cfasta_sw` and unknown names 1 (needs two sequences); `-extend_mode` unknown / `matrix` exit 1 for ANY number of sequences, `ssp` / `tm` 11 and `curvature` 1 as soon as there are two sequences (`initialize_scoring_scheme`), independent of the dp mode. Dp modes ported late in WP29: `biphasic_pair_wise` and `glocal_pair_wise` (550 sets, 544 equal; the 6 others are program aborts - exit status 6 / -6 - and one output that lost its last column, all the one-byte row overflow of the row writer: sets with alignments of 24+ columns). Dp modes that run in the program but are not ported: `gotoh_pair_wise_lgp`, `ktup_pair_wise`, `viterbi*`, `procoffee`, `co_pair_wise`, `gotoh_pair_wise_sw` (LOCAL), `test_pair_wise`, `precomputed`, `linked_pair_wise_collapse`, `sim_pair_wise_lalign` raise `NotSupportedException`; extend modes `pc`, `mixt`, `quadruplet`, `g_coffee*`, `rna1-4`, `test` have no effect on the linked DP (accepted) and raise `NotSupportedException` with a generic dp mode.
- **Tests.** `GenericDpModes_EveryRowEqualsTheProgram`, `ExtendModes_EveryRowEqualsTheProgram_RelativeTripletIsUndefinedBehaviour`, `DpAndExtendModesTheProgramRefuses_RaiseTCoffeeAbortedException`.

### F114 — ALIGN-MULTI-001 (audit A2-11): `muscle_pair` (and the real cause of the "rc 1" of `mafft_pair` / `muscle_pair`)
- **Defect / finding.** Round 1 recorded `mafft_pair` / `muscle_pair` as failing with the installed program ("its fixed command line does not suit the installed mafft / muscle 5.x"). Real cause (measured with `strace`, a logging wrapper in `PATH` and `t_coffee -debug`): `t_coffee` points `MAFFT_BINARIES` at `$HOME/.t_coffee/plugins/linux/` (and appends that directory to `PATH`); `mafft` aborts there ("tbfast / disttbfast not found", rc 1, the library stays empty: "You have not provided any method or enough Sequences [FATAL]"), and `muscle_pair` ("The Program muscle Needed by T-COFFEE Could not be found") looks for a binary named `muscle`. Fix of the ENVIRONMENT only: `generate_tcoffee_options_oracle.py` (`run_tc`, private HOME per run) links `/usr/lib/mafft/bin/*`, `/usr/lib/mafft/lib/mafft/*` (tbfast, disttbfast, ...) and `muscle` -> `/usr/bin/muscle3` into that directory.
- **Program (`util_constraints_list.c` L6416 / L7078 method files, `util_dp_drivers.c` pair jobs).** `muscle_pair`: `muscle -in IN -out OUT -quiet -maxmb 0` on every pair (ALN_MODE pairwise, OUT_MODE aln), the alignment goes through `aln2constraint_list` with the weight `sim` like every pair method; `mafft_pair`: `mafft --anysymbol IN > OUT 2>/dev/null` (FFT-NS-1 progressive alignment of the pair).
- **Port (additive API).** `TCoffeePairMethod.MusclePair` (`-method muscle_pair`): the pair alignment is `MultipleAlignMuscle` of the two sequences with the program's default `-maxiters 8` (the MUSCLE 3.8.1551 port of F65 / F70), then the shared library path (`TcPairJobEntries`). `TCoffeePairMethod.MafftPair` is parsed and raises `NotSupportedException` naming B09.md A2-19 (no MAFFT port yet): the 460 oracle sets of the program (feature `mafft_pair` / `mafft_mixed`, DNA / protein / mixed alphabets, with `slow_pair`, `proba_pair`, UPGMA, no relaxation) are committed so that the MAFFT port (A2-19) can be verified at once.
- **Reference cross-check.** `muscle_pair` / `muscle_mixed`: 460 sets (2-6 sequences of 4-36 letters; DNA, protein, mixed alphabets with letters outside the alphabets; `-method muscle_pair`, `muscle_pair,slow_pair`, `proba_pair,muscle_pair`, `-tree_mode upgma`, `-relax_lib 0`) run through the installed `t_coffee` 13.41 with the installed `muscle3` 3.8.1551: **460 / 460 rows equal** (maxiters 8; the port already matched MUSCLE 3.8 on 630 user-run sets, F65 / F70).
- **Tests.** `MusclePair_EveryRowEqualsTheProgram`, `MafftPair_OracleCommitted_PortWaitsForMafft`.

### F115 — ALIGN-MULTI-001 (audit A2-5, WP30): the dp modes `gotoh_pair_wise_lgp`, `ktup_pair_wise`, `precomputed_pair_wise`, `gotoh_pair_wise_sw`, `test_pair_wise`, `procoffee_pair_wise`, `viterbi_pair_wise` and the extend modes `pc`, `mixt`, `g_coffee`, `g_coffee_quadruplets`, `test`, `rna` / `rna1-4`
- **Program (`util_dp_gotoh_nw.c` L527-800 `aln2local_penalties` / `gotoh_pair_wise_lgp`, L248 `test_pair_wise`; `util_dp_fasta_nw.c` L1288-1420 `precomputed_pair_wise` / `ktup_pair_wise`; `util_dp_gotoh_sw.c` L141 `gotoh_pair_wise_sw`; `util_dp_drivers.c` `pair_wise`, `empty_pair_wise`, `get_pair_wise_function` (LOCAL / GLOBAL tags); `util_lib/util.c` `best_int`, `generate_null`).**
  `gotoh_pair_wise_lgp`: a three-state (M, D, I) Gotoh DP whose opening / closing penalties are position specific: `0.1 x (1 - open / n) x gop` (`float` division, `double` product, truncated; `open` = number of rows of the group that open a gap at that column, `close` likewise; zero in the first and last row / column), the extension `gep` x SCORE_K is constant, the borders `gep` x index (the other two states one lower), `dp_max` ties to the first listed state, traceback from the predecessor of the best final state (as `glocal_pair_wise`). `ktup_pair_wise` and `precomputed_pair_wise` COMPUTE A SCORE ONLY and leave every row as it is (the final rows are the input sequences, unpadded, of different length - the program writes them so); `precomputed_pair_wise` first checks `strlen (first row of group 1) == strlen (first row of group 2)` and otherwise stops ("ERROR: improper use of the function precomputed pairwise [FATAL]", exit status 1). `gotoh_pair_wise_sw` (LOCAL): the SW form of the Gotoh DP with `g = (gop - nomatch) x SCORE_K`, `h = (gep - nomatch) x SCORE_K`, a cell = the best of insertion / substitution / deletion / 0 (first strictly greater wins), the best cell = the first strictly larger score in row-major order; every row of BOTH groups is replaced by the columns of the best local alignment (the rows lose residues and become shorter than their sequences), no positive cell empties all rows, and a group with empty rows leaves the pair alone (`empty_pair_wise` of a LOCAL mode). `test_pair_wise`: no alignment, every row of group 1 gets `l1 - n` and every row of group 2 `l0 - n` gaps appended (`n = l0 / 2` for `l0 < 5`, else 5).
- **Port (additive API).** `TCoffeeDpMode.GotohLgp / Ktup / Precomputed / GotohSw / Test` (`-dp_mode gotoh_pair_wise_lgp | ktup_pair_wise | precomputed_pair_wise | gotoh_pair_wise_sw | test_pair_wise`), `TcGotohLgpMasks`, `TcLocalGapPenalties`, `TcGotohSwMasks`, `TcMergeWithoutGlobalAlignment` (`SequenceAligner.TCoffeeMethods.cs` / `TCoffeeDp.cs`); `TcGroup.Columns` is now the length of the row of the lowest sequence index of the group (as `strlen (A->seq_al[l_s[0][0]])`); `MultipleAlignTCoffeeProgram` returns rows of different lengths with an empty consensus and score 0 instead of failing in the scorer; `-dp_mode gotoh_pair_wise_lgp_sticky` (not in the dp table of the program: "unknown mode for dp_mode") joined the refused modes (40 more `dp_refused` sets, all exit status 1).
- **Reference cross-check (installed `t_coffee` 13.41, `-n_core 1 -no_warning`, `generate_tcoffee_options_oracle.py` features `dp_lgp`, `dp_sw`, `dp_ktup`, `dp_precomputed_equal`, `dp_precomputed`, `dp_test`).** `gotoh_pair_wise_lgp` **620 sets: 618 rows equal**, the 2 others are the one-byte row overflow of F113 (alignment of 24+ columns); `ktup_pair_wise` **250 / 250 equal**; `precomputed_pair_wise` **220 / 220 equal on sets of equal length, 120 / 120 sets of unequal length stop with exit status 1**; `gotoh_pair_wise_sw` **500 sets: 450 equal, 50 undefined behaviour** (exit status 6 / -6 / -9 / -11 on 49, one output differs): valgrind on the set `CVWVCITFIAFQYTADPVMHMVE|DKDGSPLSVL` (exit status 6 on every run) reports "Invalid write of size 4 ... 4 bytes after a block of size 124" inside the local alignment routine and a following invalid read: a heap overflow of the program that is not reproducible (the port returns the alignment; the test allows 10-80 such sets of 500); `test_pair_wise` **420 sets**: a merge whose second group has fewer than `n` columns calls `generate_null` with a negative size (`vcalloc` returns NULL, the terminator is written through it: 98 / 98 such sets die with exit status 11 / -11, the port raises `TCoffeeAbortedException`); otherwise the rows are written into buffers of `max_len + 1` bytes (`seq2aln`): up to one column more than the longest sequence the program writes exactly the port's rows (**50 / 50 equal**), two or more columns beyond it overflow the heap (valgrind: "Invalid write of size 1 ... in strcpy" of `sprintf (seq, "%s", buf)`): 231 of 272 such sets die, 41 differ (32 lose the last column of every row, 9 have another row clobbered).
- **Second part (same WP, commit 67d729f6).** `procoffee_pair_wise` (`util_dp_gotoh_nw.c` L2911): `cl2list_borders` FIRST, then `cl2pair_list_ext`, the older `cl2diag_cap390` over the list WITH the border cells (zero-score cells before the first cell of a diagonal segment - `delta` = the larger step from the previous cell, cells i - x, j - x skipped while i - x or j - x is at most 1 - and after the last one, skipped from the last row / column), `sort_list_int (list, 7, 1, ...)`, `list2nodup_list`, `list2linked_pair_wise` and the two groups SWAPPED (`ns[0] = nsi[1]`: the second group is profile 1, no size based swap) - **460 / 460 sets equal** (`TcSparseDp (..., cap390: true)`, `TcDiagCap390`, `TcAddBorders`). `viterbi_pair_wise` (`util_dp_suboptimal_nw.c` L1788 + `get_tot_prob2` + `aln2prf`): the Viterbi path of the five-state pair-HMM of `proba_pair` - the PROTEIN model, whatever the sequence type - between the residue FREQUENCY profiles of the two groups (`aln2prf`: the share of each of the 20 dirichlet letters among the residues of a column), single precision as the original - **420 sets, 419 equal, 1 = row-writer overflow of F113**. The extend modes (`choose_extension_mode` of `evaluate.c`, all with `slow_get_dp_cost` and another `evaluate_residue_pair`): `pc` (`residue_pair_extended_list_pc`: the sum of the smaller of two weights / 1000 (float) over the common partners divided by the smaller list length, x 1000, NOT scaled by SCORE_K), `g_coffee` (the best of the smaller weights over the common partners x SCORE_K), `g_coffee_quadruplets` (the best quadruplet; `s` keeps its last value when a pair is not common, as written), `mixt` ((quadruplet score + triplet score) x SCORE_K - both already scaled, the sum scaled again) - **760 sets, 754 equal, 6 row-writer overflows**; `quadruplet` (`get_dp_cost_quadruplet`: `slow_get_dp_cost` over `residue_pair_extended_list_quadruplet` when a group has one sequence, else `fast_get_dp_cost_quadruplet` = the two-level hashes of the partners of the partners, the larger group first; the nomatch penalty is subtracted a SECOND time) - **360 sets, 359 equal, 1 = the row-writer overflow at an intermediate merge of 8 columns (valgrind: "Invalid write of size 1 ... 0 bytes after a block of size 88"; the necessary condition of the test is now a merge of 8 mod 16 columns, i.e. a final alignment of at least 8 columns)**; `test` (`slow_get_dp_cost_test`: the raw sum of the smaller weights x 10 / (ns1 x ns2), integer division, no nomatch) and `rna` / `rna1` / `rna2` / `rna3` / `rna4` WITHOUT a contact library (`-rna_lib` is not given: R is empty, `residue_pair_extended_list4rna` returns the triplet score of the pair itself, rna4 would run RNAplfold which is not installed: the column score of rna0 = slow_triplet) - **470 sets (220 `test`, 250 `rna*`), 467 equal, 3 row-writer overflows**.
- **Not ported (DOABLE, B09.md A2-5):** `viterbiL_pair_wise` / `viterbiD_pair_wise` (the "fsa" pair-HMM of `fsa_dp.c` with MatState lists; both die on 50 % of the probe sets with exit status 11), `linked_pair_wise_collapse` (library collapse; exit status 1 on 8 of 12 probe sets), `sim_pair_wise_lalign` (exit status 6 / 11 / -11 on 9 of 12), `co_pair_wise` (= `clustalo --p1 --p2` of the installed Clustal Omega 1.2.4: depends on the Clustal Omega port A2-21), the contact libraries of `rna1-4` (`-rna_lib`, `-template_file RNA`).
- **Tests.** `GotohLgpDp_EveryRowEqualsTheProgram`, `KtupAndPrecomputedDp_EveryRowEqualsTheProgram`, `LocalAndTestDp_EveryRowEqualsTheProgram` (the abort / overflow conditions of `gotoh_pair_wise_sw` and `test_pair_wise` are asserted on every committed set through the internal `TcMergeTrace`), `ProcoffeeDp_EveryRowEqualsTheProgram`, `ViterbiDp_EveryRowEqualsTheProgram`, `PcMixtAndGCoffeeExtendModes_EveryRowEqualsTheProgram`, `QuadrupletExtendMode_EveryRowEqualsTheProgram`, `TestAndRnaExtendModes_EveryRowEqualsTheProgram`, `DpAndExtendModesTheProgramRefuses_RaiseTCoffeeAbortedException` (+ `gotoh_pair_wise_lgp_sticky`, `viterbiL_pair_wise` still NotSupported).

### F116 — ALIGN-MULTI-001 (audit A2-10 / A2-12): the structure methods `TMalign_pair`, `mustang_pair`, `sap_pair` / `-mode 3dcoffee`, `dali_pair`, `fugue_pair`
- **Round-1 proof challenged and corrected.** "They need PDB structures, not sequences" is solvable: synthetic CA-only PDB files (a random-walk or helix backbone; related members share it with insertions, deletions, substitutions, a rigid motion and Gaussian coordinate noise; `generate_tcoffee_external_oracle.py`) are handed to `t_coffee` as `_P_` templates (`>name _P_ file` in a `-template_file`; `is_pdb_file` needs a line break before `HEADER` or `SEQRES` and before `ATOM`, otherwise "Could Not Fill _P_ template"). TM-align (apt `tm-align` 20190822) and MUSTANG 3.2.4 (apt `mustang`) are installed. The structure aligner is the EXTERNAL program and is not reimplemented; T-Coffee's side is ported and verified with what the aligner wrote as input.
- **Program (`util_dp_drivers.c` `pdb_pair` L1757, `tc_generic_method.pl` `seq2pdb_pair` (script extracted from the running binary), `aln_convertion_util.c` `fill_P_template` L6506, `util_constraints_list.c` L5832-6040 method files and `check_seq_type`, `method_file2...` L840-870).** `fill_P_template` attaches a PDB sequence to a sequence only when `align_two_sequences (seq, pdb, "idmat", -3, 0, "fasta_pair_wise")` (lower case, k-tuple 3, terminal gaps free) has `aln2sim (idmat) > 35` and `aln2coverage > 50`. The method files say `SEQ_TYPE P`: a pair whose two sequences do not both carry a template is NOT skipped but aligned by `proba_pair` ("Method TMalign cannot be applied to [a vs b], proba_pair will be used instead"). TMalign: the glue keeps `tail -4` of the standard output, reverses the lines (l[3] = the first of the four, l[1] = the third), replaces every character outside `[a-zA-Z0-9-]` by '-' - the line break included, so each row gets one extra '-' - and the result is read as FASTA; mustang: `mustang -i pdb1 pdb2 -F fasta`, the file `results.afasta`, kept ONLY when mustang exits with status 0 (it segfaults, exit status -11, on some pairs AFTER writing the file; the alignment of such a pair is lost: found because 1 of the first 160 mustang sets differed); either alignment goes through `aln2constraint_list (F, CL, "sim")`. Environment: the private `$HOME/.t_coffee/plugins/linux` links `TMalign` and `mustang`.
  **SAP / Dali / FUGUE.** `sap_pair` and `-mode 3dcoffee` (`get_3dcoffee_defaults`: `-in Msap_pair -template_file SELF_P_ -profile_template_file SELF_P_`): `util_constraints_list.c` L5904 intercepts `sap_pair` when SAP is not installed ("SAP is not installed. TMalign will be used instead"): the method becomes `pdb_pair` + TMalign - so the installed program computes TM-align on `sap_pair`; `-mode 3dcoffee` adds it to the default `proba_pair` (`-in` ADDS) and also makes the program look the sequence NAMES up on the network (`tc_generic_method.pl -netfile qNNN`, no network here: about 15 s per sequence). `dali_pair` (`pdb_pair` + `EXECUTABLE2 DaliLite`): the first pair of two usable structures stops the program ("ERROR: UNSPECIFIED UNSPECIFIED", exit status 1), pairs without two structures use `proba_pair`; `fugue_pair`: "The Program fugueali Needed by T-COFFEE Could not be found [FATAL:T-COFFEE]" at once.
- **Port (additive API).** `TCoffeePairMethod.TMalignPair / MustangPair / SapPair / DaliPair / FuguePair`; `TCoffeeProgramOptions.StructureTemplates` (the PDB sequence of every sequence, `null` / empty = none) and `StructureAlignerOutputs` (per method, keyed by the input indices of the two sequences in the order the program calls the aligner: the standard output of TMalign / the content of `results.afasta`; a missing pair = the aligner failed: no entries); `SequenceAligner.TCoffeeTMalignAlignment` / `TCoffeeMustangAlignment` (the glue parsers, public), internal `TcPdbTemplateUsable` (`fill_P_template`'s test, reusing `TcFastaPairRows`, `TcSeqSimMeasure`); `-mode 3dcoffee` / `-special_mode 3dcoffee` / `-mode expresso` in `FromCommandLine` raise `NotSupportedException` (3dcoffee: see above; expresso downloads the structures from the RCSB); MCP `multiple_align_tcoffee`: `structureTemplates`, `structureAlignerOutputs`, `blastXml`. `SapPair` runs the TMalign path (the real SAP: hand-off package `handoff/A2-12-sap-dali`).
- **Reference cross-check (installed `t_coffee` 13.41 + `TMalign` / `mustang`).** `TMalign_pair` **160 / 160** sets (2-6 sequences of 14-40 residues; 15 % of the sequences without a structure, 15 % with a structure whose sequence differs, 10 % of the structures short; `TMalign_pair`, `,slow_pair`, `proba_pair,`, `-relax_lib 0`, `-tree_mode upgma`), `mustang_pair` **160 / 160** (also with `TMalign_pair` in the same method list), `sap_pair` **150 / 150** (= TMalign), `-mode 3dcoffee` **7 / 8 sets equal `-method proba_pair,sap_pair`** (the eighth: 5 sequences, the program's library holds fewer pair contributions - CONS 1 where `proba_pair,sap_pair` gives 2 on ~900 characters of the library - cause not understood; the mode raises `NotSupportedException` instead of claiming equality; DOABLE sub-item: find the cause, the sets are committed), `dali_pair` **60 / 60** (56 stops at the first pair of two usable structures, 4 sets run through with proba_pair), `fugue_pair` **60 / 60 stops**. One of the first 160 mustang sets differed until the exit-status rule of the glue (above) was found; every set is equal now.
- **Blocked (proof):** `-mode expresso` / `-template_file EXPRESSO` fetch the structures from the RCSB PDB / EBI by BLAST against the PDB (network; no PDB mirror is reachable: `ncbi.nlm.nih.gov` and `rcsb.org` are blocked) - not reproducible offline; `fugueali` (FUGUE) has no obtainable source: `install.pl` of T-Coffee lists its source as "empty", `http://www-cryst.bioc.cam.ac.uk/fugue/download.html` is unreachable, PyPI `fugue` is the unrelated fugue-project, bioconda has no fugue, `sources.debian.org/api/src/{sap,dalilite}` = 404, GitHub search `fugueali` / `FUGUE homstrad` = no repository. SAP and DaliLite ARE obtainable (bioconda `sap` 1.1.3 = `https://github.com/mathbio-nimr-mrc-ac-uk/SAP/archive/v.1.1.3.tar.gz`, sha256 1ee5025f...; `https://github.com/liisaholm/DaliLite.v5`) but the sandbox refused to run the downloaded binary (classifier): hand-off package `docs/Validation/review-2026-09/handoff/A2-12-sap-dali/` (Dockerfile, run.sh, README, `scripts/alignment/generate_tcoffee_external_oracle.py` feature `sap_real`, `dali_probe.py`) - the item stays DOABLE-awaiting-user for the real SAP / DaliLite glue.
- **Tests.** `TMalignPair_EveryRowEqualsTheProgram`, `MustangPair_EveryRowEqualsTheProgram`, `SapPair_IsTMalignWhenSapIsNotInstalled` (incl. the 7 / 8 of `-mode 3dcoffee`), `DaliAndFuguePair_TheProgramStopsBecauseTheAlignersAreNotInstalled`, `TMalignAlignment_IsTheFirstAndThirdOfTheLastFourLines_EachWithAFinalGap`, `MustangAlignment_IsTheFirstTwoFastaRecords`.

### F117 — ALIGN-MULTI-001 (audit A2-9): `blastp_msa` from the BLAST HSPs of a local database
- **Round-1 proof challenged and corrected.** "No database, legacy_blast.pl missing" is solvable locally: `blastp`, `makeblastdb` (ncbi-blast+ 2.12.0) are installed, `apt-get install ncbi-blast+-legacy` provides `blastall` / `formatdb` and `/usr/bin/legacy_blast` (Debian names it without `.pl`; `t_coffee` looks for `legacy_blast.pl` in its private plug-in directory: the oracle script links it there). `blastp_msa` needs no external database at all: the program formats the INPUT SEQUENCES as the database (`seq2blastdb`: `makeblastdb -dbtype prot`) and searches each of them against it (`blastall -i seq.pep -d db.pep -m7 -p blastp`). The BLAST search is the external program and is not reimplemented; T-Coffee's side (library construction from the XML) is ported and verified with the XML BLAST wrote as input.
- **Program (`util_dp_drivers.c` `seq_msa` L1810, `tc_generic_method.pl` `blast_msa`, `blast_xml2profile`, `ncbi_blast_xml2profile`, `xml2tag_list`, `util_constraints_list.c` `read_constraint_list_file` L2778, `add_entry2list`).** For every `Iteration` (query i) and every `Hit` (definition = sequence j; an unknown name is order undef + 1 = 1) and every `Hsp`: `# i j`, then for every column with a residue in both rows `Qstart-1  Hstart-1  identity` after the residue counters were advanced, `identity` = `%d` of `Hsp_identity / Hsp_align-len x 100` (a double, truncated; 0 when the length is 0); `maxid` 100, `minid` 0, `mincov` 0 keep every HSP. The library file is read by `read_constraint_list_file`: lines with weight 0 are skipped, `add_entry2list` adds the entry and its mirror (weight = max, CONS += 0); self hits (i = j) are added too (they take part in the relaxation). `xml2tag_list` is a token scanner (the text between the tags and the tags themselves are appended to the body of the open element; a tag is recognised by the substrings `<tag ` / `<tag>` and `/tag>`), reproduced as such. Note the CONS count of a library file entry is 0 (not 1 as for alignments).
- **Port (additive API).** `TCoffeePairMethod.BlastpMsa` (`-method blastp_msa`; ONE job for all sequences, ALN_MODE multiple, in the process structure of `TcRunMethod`), `TCoffeeProgramOptions.BlastXml` (names default to q000, q001, ... by input index, else `Names`), public `SequenceAligner.TCoffeeBlastMsaLibraryEntries(names, sequences, blastXml)` (the library entries as printed by `-out_lib`), internal `TcXmlTagList`, `TcBlastMsaEntries`; MCP `multiple_align_tcoffee` parameter `blastXml`.
- **Reference cross-check.** 170 sets (2-8 protein sequences of 25-70 residues, 70 % related variants with substitutions / insertions / deletions; `-method blastp_msa`, `,slow_pair`, `proba_pair,`, `-relax_lib 0`, `-tree_mode upgma`): the installed `t_coffee` rows (**170 / 170 equal**) and the library `-lib_only -out_lib` of the 85 sets with the plain method (**85 / 85 equal**, > 1 000 entries compared: pairs, weights, counts) from the XML `blastall -m7` wrote on the same sequences (committed zlib+base64 per set in `tcoffee_external_oracle.tsv`, 1.7 MB in all). A first version of the token scanner did not reset the body between elements (every hit then contained its predecessors): the library test found it (229 entries instead of 186).
- **`tblastx_msa` (same item, ported in the same WP, commit 67d729f6).** `tc_generic_method.pl` `seq2tblastx_lib`: `formatdb -i infile -p F` (nucleotide database), `blastall -p tblastx -i infile -d infile -m 7 -S1`, then `ncbi_tblastx_xml2lib_file`: per `Iteration` (query = `Iteration_iter-num`, `Iteration_query-len`) and per `Hit` of a LATER sequence (`Hit_accession + 1 > query`; hits of earlier sequences and self hits are skipped and print no header), `# qindex hindex`, then for every nucleotide `e = rqs .. rqe - 1` of the query range of every HSP the entry `(e, rhs + d)` with the weight `int (Hsp_identity x 100 / Hsp_align-len)` and CONS 0 (frame <= 0: `rqe = qlen - qs + 1`, `rqs = qlen - qe + 1`, hits likewise; the range end itself is NOT included). Port: `TCoffeePairMethod.TblastxMsa` (`-method tblastx_msa`, one job, `TCoffeeProgramOptions.BlastXml`), public `SequenceAligner.TCoffeeTblastxLibraryEntries`, internal `TcTblastxEntries`. Reference cross-check: 106 sets (2-6 DNA sequences of 60-150 nt, 75 % codon-preserving variants of one base sequence with point mutations and 3-nt indels; `-method tblastx_msa`, `,slow_pair`, `proba_pair,`, `-relax_lib 0`, `-tree_mode upgma`): **101 / 101 rows equal, 5 sets without any hit stop the program (empty library; `TCoffeeAbortedException`), the libraries of the plain-method sets equal on every set (> 1 000 entries)**. Test `TblastxMsa_EveryRowAndLibraryEqualsTheProgram`.
- **Not done (A2-9 remainder, DOABLE):** `blastp_o2a`, `tblastpx_msa` (`seq2tblastpx_lib` / `ncbi_tblastpx_xml2lib_file`: the six-frame translations as protein sequences, `tblastpx_name2description`, `tblastpx_hsp2coordinates`), `-prot_min_sim` / `-prot_max_sim` / `-prot_min_cov` and psicoffee (`-template_file BLAST`: `psiblast` profiles as `_R_` templates, `seq2profile_template`, the profile-aware `proba_pair`).
- **Tests.** `BlastpMsa_EveryRowEqualsTheProgram`, `BlastpMsa_LibraryEqualsTheProgram`.

### F118 — ALIGN-MULTI-001 (audit A2-20, WP31): Kalign 3.3.5 ported; rows equal the installed program on 526 / 526 runs, command lines on 83 / 83
- **Finding.** No Kalign in the library. Source actually opened: `kalign_3.3.5.orig.tar.gz` + `kalign_3.3.5-1.debian.tar.xz` (deb.debian.org; read as text, nothing compiled or run); the oracle is the installed `kalign` 1:3.3.5-1 (SIMD dispatcher -> `kalign-avx2`).
- **Port (additive API).** `SequenceAligner.MultipleAlignKalign(sequences, KalignOptions)` / `KalignRun` (`SequenceAligner.Kalign.cs`, `SequenceAligner.KalignTables.cs`): alphabet detection from the letter counts, the 13-letter reduced protein alphabet and ACGTN, Myers' bit-parallel distance on 256-bit words (`bpm_256`), UPGMA (< 100 sequences) / bisecting 2-means (>= 100) guide tree with `edist_256`'s lane order and glibc's merge sort for the anchors, progressive profile alignment (`aln_seqseq / seqprofile / profileprofile`, Hirschberg divide and conquer with the program's meet-up tie-break, `update_n`, `make_seq`), the four matrices and gap defaults; `KalignOptions` (`Type`, `GapOpen`, `GapExtend`, `TerminalGapExtend`, `Threads`); command line `KalignProgram.Parse / Run` (getopt_long_only forms, `atof` / `atoi`, the `strstr` type word, FASTA in / out with 60 columns); MCP `multiple_align_kalign`, `kalign_command` (56 Alignment tools, catalog regenerated and `--check`ed, README, MCP_STATUS, traceability, tool-map, EXPECTED_COUNTS 520).
- **Quirks of 3.3.5 found in the source and confirmed by the binary (all reproduced):** `--tgpe` is applied only when `--gpe` is given (`aln_param.c` tests gpe twice); `--type internal` = RNA (`strstr(in, "rna")` matches first; `KalignType.DnaInternal` is library-only and checked as DNA with `--gpo 8 --gpe 6 --tgpe 8`); `add_gap_info_to_path_n` never sets the gap-open flags (dead loop), so inserted profile columns cost extensions only; `-n 1` runs `do_align_serial` (no shorter-first swap) - rows differ from `-n 4` on 17 of 48 sets; `aln_runner` falls through into the serial runner below 500 columns; detection of DNA vs protein is statistical (short peptides of A, C, G, T only are DNA); the pattern of the AVX2 distance is cut at 255 residues (the portable build: 1 024).
- **Reference cross-check.** `kalign_oracle.tsv` (scripts/alignment/generate_kalign_oracle.py): 526 runs (DNA / RNA / protein, every `--type`, every gap option, `-n 1 / 2 / 4`, > 500 columns, > 255 residues, 99 ... 1 000 sequences, 5 refusals): **526 / 526 equal**. `kalign_command_oracle.tsv`: **83 / 83** command lines equal (stdout / `-o` text and exit status; 27 refusals incl. the program's segfault on one sequence, reported as exit status 1).
- **Not done (DOABLE, registered A2-20b):** `--format msf` / `clu` output and MSF / Clustal input of the command line (`write_msa_msf` with the GCG checksum, `write_msa_clu`, `read_msf`, `read_clu`); the timestamped log lines of `-n 1` / non-quiet runs; the portable-build `bpm_block` distance (pattern limit 1 024) is not selectable.
- **Tests.** `SequenceAligner_KalignOracle_Tests` (527), `SequenceAligner_KalignProgram_Tests` (88), `SequenceAligner_Kalign_Tests` (25), MCP `KalignTests` (4). Commit c17ff400.

### F119 — ALIGN-MULTI-001 (audit A2-19, WP31): MAFFT 7.505 stage 1 - the alignment of two sequences ported; `mafft_pair` of T-Coffee wired; 884 / 884 pairs and 460 / 460 T-Coffee sets equal the installed programs
- **Finding.** No MAFFT in the library; T-Coffee's `mafft_pair` (`mafft --anysymbol IN > OUT` on every pair) raised `NotSupportedException`. Sources actually opened: `mafft_7.505.orig.tar.gz` (deb.debian.org; read as text, nothing compiled or run): `core/mafft.tmpl`, `disttbfast.c`, `constants.c`, `blosum.c`, `Galign11.c`, `replaceu.c`, `io.c`, `mtxutl.c`; oracle: the installed `mafft` 7.505-1 (and `disttbfast -d`, which prints the scoring matrix).
- **Scope decision (user rule: land verified stages, register the exact remainder).** The full MAFFT is ~55 000 lines of C over a dozen programs (`disttbfast`, `pairlocalalign`, `dvtditr`, `Falign` + `fft`, `A__align`, `Lalign11`, `Galign11`, `genalign11`, ...); the first verified stage is the alignment of TWO sequences - exactly what `mafft_pair` needs - because for a pair every default strategy (`--retree 1 / 2`, `--fft`, `--nofft`) reaches `G__align11` (measured: rows identical to `--nofft` on 884 / 884 pairs up to 1 500 residues).
- **Port (additive API).** `SequenceAligner.MultipleAlignMafft(sequences, MafftOptions)` (`SequenceAligner.Mafft.cs`, `SequenceAligner.MafftTables.cs`): `replaceu` / `getnumlen` front end (alphabet guess, `--anysymbol` replacement and restoration, plain command), `constants()` scoring matrices (BLOSUM `--bl` 30 ... 0, Kimura 200-PAM for nucleotides, IUPAC averaging) with the integer penalties (`--op`, `--ep` = offset), free end gaps (`-O`), `G__align11` + `Atracking`; MCP `multiple_align_mafft` (57 Alignment tools, catalog regenerated and `--check`ed, README, MCP_STATUS, traceability, tool-map, EXPECTED_COUNTS 521); T-Coffee `TCoffeePairMethod.MafftPair` now aligns each pair with it.
- **Bug found while porting (mine, caught by the oracle before commit).** `blosum.c` defines `tmpmtx62` twice (`#if 0 ... #else ...`); the first extraction took the dead one: 76 of 885 pairs differed (ties and a few "better-scoring" gaps) until the matrix equalled `disttbfast -d`'s print cell for cell.
- **Reference cross-check.** `mafft_pair_oracle.tsv` (scripts/alignment/generate_mafft_pair_oracle.py): 884 pairs (220 protein, 200 DNA / RNA, 160 with IUPAC codes / unusual symbols, mixed alphabets, `-` in the input, `--op`, `--ep`, `--bl` x 8, `--nuc` / `--amino`, `--retree`, `--fft`, `--nofft`, `--reorder`, 60 plain-command pairs, 47 pairs of 400 - 1 500 residues): **884 / 884 equal**; `mafft_pair` / `mafft_mixed` of `tcoffee_program_options_oracle.tsv`: **460 / 460 equal**.
- **Not done (DOABLE, registered A2-19b / A2-19c).** Three or more sequences: `distancematrixthread` 6-mer distance, guide tree (`--retree`, `fastconstruction`), `treebase` with the group-group `A__align` (`Dalignmm.c`), `Falign.c` + `fft.c` (FFT-NS), `--reorder`, `--6merpair`, `--parttree` / `--dpparttree` / `--fastaparttree`, `--jtt` / `--tm` (`constants.c` JTT branch, `JTT.c`), `--lop` / `--lep`; iterative refinement `--maxiterate` (`dvtditr.c`, `tditeration.c`, `treeOperation.c`), `--localpair` (`pairlocalalign.c`, `Lalign11.c`, the `hat3` library, `Falign_localhom.c`), `--globalpair` (`Galign11.c` pair library), `--genafpair` (`genalign11.c`), `--auto` (the script's strategy rule).
- **Tests.** `SequenceAligner_MafftPair_Tests` (886), `SequenceAligner_Mafft_Tests` (6), T-Coffee `MafftPair_EveryRowEqualsTheProgram`, MCP `MafftTests` (2). Commit c17ff400.

### F120 — ALIGN-MULTI-001 (audit A2-27, WP32): the 29 EMBOSS `-aformat` alignment output formats written byte for byte
- **Finding.** `FormatAlignment` printed one three-line layout; no EMBOSS `-aformat` family (pair, markx0-10, srspair, msf, clustal, mega, nexus, phylip, selex, sam, tcoffee ...) existed.
